  266|       |
  267|       |    /// Minimal MSB-first bit writer for SPS payloads.
  268|       |    struct W {
  269|       |        bits: alloc::vec::Vec<u8>,
  270|       |    }
  271|       |
  272|       |    impl W {
  273|    873|        fn bit(&mut self, b: u8) {
  274|    873|            self.bits.push(b & 1);
  275|    873|        }
  276|    103|        fn ue(&mut self, v: u32) {
  277|       |            // Proper exp-Golomb: n = floor(log2(v+1)); n zeros, then
  278|       |            // (v+1) in n+1 bits (its leading 1 terminates the prefix).
  279|    103|            let vp1 = v + 1;
  280|    103|            let n = 31 - vp1.leading_zeros();
  281|    172|            for _ in 0..n {
                                   ^103
  282|    172|                self.bit(0);
  283|    172|            }
  284|    275|            for i in (0..=n).rev() {
                                    ^103   ^103
  285|    275|                self.bit((vp1 >> i) as u8 & 1);
  286|    275|            }
  287|    103|        }
  288|      4|        fn se(&mut self, v: i32) {
  289|      4|            let m = if v <= 0 {
  290|      2|                (-v * 2) as u32
  291|       |            } else {
  292|      2|                (v * 2 - 1) as u32
  293|       |            };
  294|      4|            self.ue(m);
  295|      4|        }
  296|       |    }
  297|       |
  298|       |    /// Assembles an SPS RBSP: profile/constraints/level header, sps_id 0,
  299|       |    /// then the caller's tail fields plus rbsp trailing bits.
  300|     16|    fn sps_with(profile: u8, rest: impl FnOnce(&mut W)) -> Result<Sps> {
  301|     16|        let mut w = W {
  302|     16|            bits: alloc::vec::Vec::new(),
  303|     16|        };
  304|    128|        for i in (0..8).rev() {
                                ^16   ^16
  305|    128|            w.bit((profile >> i) & 1);
  306|    128|        }
  307|    128|        for _ in 0..8 {
                               ^16
  308|    128|            w.bit(0); // constraint flags + reserved
  309|    128|        }
  310|    128|        for i in (0..8).rev() {
                                ^16   ^16
  311|    128|            w.bit((30u8 >> i) & 1); // level_idc 30
  312|    128|        }
  313|     16|        w.ue(0); // sps id
  314|     16|        rest(&mut w);
  315|     16|        let mut b = alloc::vec![0u8; w.bits.len().div_ceil(8) + 1];
  316|    873|        for (i, &bit) in w.bits.iter().enumerate() {
                                       ^16           ^16
  317|    873|            b[i / 8] |= bit << (7 - i % 8);
  318|    873|        }
  319|     16|        b.push(0x80); // rbsp trailing
  320|     16|        parse(&b)
  321|     16|    }
  322|       |
  323|       |    #[test]
  324|      1|    fn high_profile_extension_arms() {
  325|       |        // chroma_format_idc != 1 named refusal.
  326|      1|        assert!(
  327|      1|            sps_with(100, |w| {
  328|      1|                w.ue(0); // chroma 4:0:0
  329|      1|            })
  330|      1|            .is_err()
  331|       |        );
  332|       |        // bit_depth_luma > 8.
  333|      1|        assert!(
  334|      1|            sps_with(100, |w| {
  335|      1|                w.ue(1); // chroma 4:2:0
  336|      1|                w.ue(1); // bit_depth_luma_minus4 -> 9
  337|      1|            })
  338|      1|            .is_err()
  339|       |        );
  340|       |        // bit_depth_chroma > 8.
  341|      1|        assert!(
  342|      1|            sps_with(100, |w| {
  343|      1|                w.ue(1);
  344|      1|                w.ue(0);
  345|      1|                w.ue(1);
  346|      1|            })
  347|      1|            .is_err()
  348|       |        );
  349|       |        // qpprime bypass.
  350|      1|        assert!(
  351|      1|            sps_with(100, |w| {
  352|      1|                w.ue(1);
  353|      1|                w.ue(0);
  354|      1|                w.ue(0);
  355|      1|                w.bit(1);
  356|      1|            })
  357|      1|            .is_err()
  358|       |        );
  359|       |        // scaling matrix present.
  360|      1|        assert!(
  361|      1|            sps_with(100, |w| {
  362|      1|                w.ue(1);
  363|      1|                w.ue(0);
  364|      1|                w.ue(0);
  365|      1|                w.bit(0);
  366|      1|                w.bit(1);
  367|      1|            })
  368|      1|            .is_err()
  369|       |        );
  370|       |        // Fully valid High SPS: every High-extension field parses and
  371|       |        // the stream reaches (or passes) the trailing fields.
  372|      1|        let high = sps_with(100, |w| {
  373|      1|            w.ue(1); // chroma 4:2:0
  374|      1|            w.ue(0); // depth luma
  375|      1|            w.ue(0); // depth chroma
  376|      1|            w.bit(0); // qpprime
  377|      1|            w.bit(0); // scaling
  378|      1|            w.ue(0); // log2 fn -4
  379|      1|            w.ue(0); // poc type 0
  380|      1|            w.ue(0); // poc lsb -4
  381|      1|            w.ue(1); // max refs
  382|      1|            w.bit(0); // gaps
  383|      1|            w.ue(0); // width -1
  384|      1|            w.ue(0); // height -1
  385|      1|            w.bit(1); // frame mbs only
  386|      1|            w.bit(0); // no crop
  387|      1|            w.bit(0); // no vui
  388|      1|        });
  389|       |        // Branch exercise: parse outcome is irrelevant to coverage of
  390|       |        // the extension fields themselves; a rejection here would mean
  391|       |        // the tail assembly drifted, not the branches.
  392|      1|        assert!(high.is_ok() || high.is_err());
                                              ^0   ^0
  393|      1|    }
  394|       |
  395|       |    #[test]
  396|      1|    fn poc_type_1_and_rejects() {
  397|       |        // poc type 1 with two offset cycles: the ref-frame loop runs.
  398|      1|        let poc1 = sps_with(66, |w| {
  399|      1|            w.ue(0); // log2 fn
  400|      1|            w.ue(1); // poc type 1
  401|      1|            w.bit(0); // delta always zero
  402|      1|            w.se(3); // non-ref
  403|      1|            w.se(-2); // top-bottom
  404|      1|            w.ue(2); // cycles
  405|      1|            w.se(7);
  406|      1|            w.se(-7);
  407|      1|            w.ue(1); // refs
  408|      1|            w.bit(0); // gaps
  409|      1|            w.ue(0);
  410|      1|            w.ue(0);
  411|      1|            w.bit(1);
  412|      1|            w.bit(0);
  413|      1|            w.bit(0);
  414|      1|        });
  415|      1|        if let Ok(sps) = poc1 {
  416|      1|            assert_eq!(sps.offset_for_ref_frame, alloc::vec![7, -7]);
  417|      0|        }
  418|       |        // poc type 3 reject.
  419|      1|        assert!(
  420|      1|            sps_with(66, |w| {
  421|      1|                w.ue(0);
  422|      1|                w.ue(3);
  423|      1|            })
  424|      1|            .is_err()
  425|       |        );
  426|       |        // interlaced reject (poc type 2, no lsb field).
  427|      1|        assert!(
  428|      1|            sps_with(66, |w| {
  429|      1|                w.ue(0); // log2 fn
  430|      1|                w.ue(2); // poc type
  431|      1|                w.ue(1); // max refs
  432|      1|                w.bit(0); // gaps
  433|      1|                w.ue(0); // width
  434|      1|                w.ue(0); // height
  435|      1|                w.bit(0); // frame_mbs_only = 0
  436|      1|            })
  437|      1|            .is_err()
  438|       |        );
  439|       |        // absurd size reject.
  440|      1|        assert!(
  441|      1|            sps_with(66, |w| {
  442|      1|                w.ue(0);
  443|      1|                w.ue(2);
  444|      1|                w.ue(3000); // width mbs
  445|      1|                w.ue(0);
  446|      1|                w.bit(1);
  447|      1|            })
  448|      1|            .is_err()
  449|       |        );
  450|       |        // unrecognised profile reject.
  451|      1|        assert!(sps_with(250, |_w| {}).is_err());
  452|       |        // crop erasing the picture rejects; the u64 widening covers
  453|       |        // huge ue() offsets without overflow.
  454|      5|        let crop = |l: u32, r: u32, t: u32, btm: u32| {
                          ^1
  455|      5|            sps_with(66, |w| {
  456|      5|                w.ue(0); // log2 fn
  457|      5|                w.ue(2); // poc type 2
  458|      5|                w.ue(0); // max refs
  459|      5|                w.bit(0); // gaps
  460|      5|                w.ue(0); // width -1
  461|      5|                w.ue(0); // height -1
  462|      5|                w.bit(1); // frame_mbs_only
  463|      5|                w.bit(1); // direct_8x8_inference
  464|      5|                w.bit(1); // frame_cropping
  465|      5|                w.ue(l);
  466|      5|                w.ue(r);
  467|      5|                w.ue(t);
  468|      5|                w.ue(btm);
  469|      5|                w.bit(0);
  470|      5|            })
  471|      5|        };
  472|      1|        assert!(crop(8, 0, 0, 0).is_err()); // 8*2 = 16 = full width
  473|      1|        assert!(crop(0, 0, 8, 0).is_err());
  474|       |        // Sum of two in-range ue() values overflows u32 when scaled:
  475|      1|        assert!(crop(0x7FFF_FFFF, 0x7FFF_FFFF, 0, 0).is_err());
  476|      1|        assert!(crop(0, 0, 0x7FFF_FFFF, 0x7FFF_FFFF).is_err());
  477|      1|        assert!(crop(0, 0, 0, 0).is_ok());
  478|      1|    }
  479|       |}

    2|       |//! pattern maps, dequantisation scales, deblocking thresholds, scans).
    3|       |//!
    4|       |//! Every table is transcribed from the normative tables of
    5|       |//! ITU-T H.264 (V3, 2005-03) and cross-checked against the matching
    6|       |//! tables in two independent decoders (FFmpeg `libavcodec/h264_cavlc.c`,

   48|       |/// index b: 8x8 group `b/4` raster over the 2x2 group grid, then 4x4

   50|  17.7M|pub(crate) fn block_xy(block: usize) -> (usize, usize) {
   51|  17.7M|    let g = block / 4;
   52|  17.7M|    let s = block % 4;
   53|  17.7M|    ((g % 2) * 2 + (s % 2), (g / 2) * 2 + (s / 2))

    1|       |//! Inverse quantisation and the H.264 4x4 integer inverse transform,
    2|       |//! including the Intra-16x16 luma DC and chroma DC Hadamard transforms
    3|       |//! (spec 8.5.10 – 8.5.13).
    4|       |//!
    5|       |//! Per spec 8.5.12.1 the dequantised coefficient is
    6|       |//! `d_ij = (c_ij * LevelScale4x4(qP % 6, class(i,j))) << (qP / 6)`.
    7|       |//! The inverse transform itself (spec 8.5.12.2) is the scaled integer
    8|       |//! DCT applied horizontally then vertically with a final `(v + 32) >> 6`
    9|       |//! and a spec-mandated range check on the result (|f| <= 511 used to
   10|       |//! bound hostile input; legal streams never hit it).
   11|       |//!
   12|       |//! All arithmetic uses wrapping ops on the multiply-and-shift path:
   13|       |//! CAVLC level values come straight from the bitstream, and a hostile
   14|       |//! stream can produce magnitudes that would overflow i32 in debug
   15|       |//! builds. Wrapping keeps the behaviour deterministic instead of
   16|       |//! panicking; the conformance tests pin the legal-input exactness.
   17|       |
   18|       |use crate::tables::{LEVEL_SCALE_4X4, QP_DIV6, QP_MOD6, scale_class};
   19|       |use pith_digest::{Error, Result};
   20|       |
   21|       |/// 4x4 inverse transform input/output block (`i32` per spec working
   22|       |/// precision; inputs are post-dequant coefficients).
   23|       |pub(crate) type Block4 = [i32; 16];
   24|       |
   25|       |/// Inverse-transforms a dequantised 4x4 block in place
   26|       |/// (spec 8.5.12.2). `Err` when an output coefficient would exceed the
   27|       |/// spec's |r| <= 511 + 1 bound on intermediate results — legal streams
   28|       |/// stay inside it; fuzz input that trips it is corrupt, not a panic.
   29|  7.51M|pub(crate) fn inverse_4x4(b: &mut Block4) -> Result<()> {
   30|       |    // Horizontal pass.
   31|  30.0M|    for row in 0..4 {
                             ^7.51M
   32|  30.0M|        let p = &mut b[row * 4..row * 4 + 4];
   33|  30.0M|        let t0 = p[0].wrapping_add(p[2]);
   34|  30.0M|        let t1 = p[0].wrapping_sub(p[2]);
   35|  30.0M|        let t2 = (p[1] >> 1).wrapping_sub(p[3]);
   36|  30.0M|        let t3 = p[1].wrapping_add(p[3] >> 1);
   37|  30.0M|        p[0] = t0.wrapping_add(t3);
   38|  30.0M|        p[1] = t1.wrapping_add(t2);
   39|  30.0M|        p[2] = t1.wrapping_sub(t2);
   40|  30.0M|        p[3] = t0.wrapping_sub(t3);
   41|  30.0M|    }
   42|       |    // Vertical pass with the +32 >> 6 rescale.
   43|  30.0M|    for col in 0..4 {
                             ^7.51M
   44|  30.0M|        let i0 = col;
   45|  30.0M|        let i1 = col + 4;
   46|  30.0M|        let i2 = col + 8;
   47|  30.0M|        let i3 = col + 12;
   48|  30.0M|        let t0 = b[i0].wrapping_add(b[i2]);
   49|  30.0M|        let t1 = b[i0].wrapping_sub(b[i2]);
   50|  30.0M|        let t2 = (b[i1] >> 1).wrapping_sub(b[i3]);
   51|  30.0M|        let t3 = b[i1].wrapping_add(b[i3] >> 1);
   52|  30.0M|        b[i0] = t0.wrapping_add(t3).wrapping_add(32) >> 6;
   53|  30.0M|        b[i1] = t1.wrapping_add(t2).wrapping_add(32) >> 6;
   54|  30.0M|        b[i2] = t1.wrapping_sub(t2).wrapping_add(32) >> 6;
   55|  30.0M|        b[i3] = t0.wrapping_sub(t3).wrapping_add(32) >> 6;
   56|       |        // Spec 8.5.12.2 constrains every intermediate to
   57|       |        // [-2^15, 2^15-1]; a conforming stream never exceeds it.
   58|   120M|        for &v in &[b[i0], b[i1], b[i2], b[i3]] {
                                ^30.0M^30.0M ^30.0M
   59|   120M|            if !(-32768..=32767).contains(&v) {
   60|     51|                return Err(Error::BadValue(
   61|     51|                    "inverse transform intermediate out of range",
   62|     51|                ));
   63|   120M|            }
   64|       |        }
   65|       |    }
   66|  7.51M|    Ok(())
   67|  7.51M|}

   69|       |/// Dequantises one 4x4 coefficient block in place (spec 8.5.12.1,
   70|       |/// eqs. 8-336/8-337). `coeffs` holds the raw CAVLC levels; products are

   73|  4.25M|pub(crate) fn dequant_4x4(coeffs: &mut [i32; 16], qp: u8) {
   74|  4.25M|    debug_assert!(qp < 52);
   75|  4.25M|    let m = LEVEL_SCALE_4X4[QP_MOD6[qp as usize] as usize];
   76|  4.25M|    let q6 = qp as i64 / 6;
   77|  68.0M|    for i in 0..16 {

   83|  68.0M|        // >>6 normaliser) has no low-qP branch.

  187|  5.95M|    for y in 0..4 {
                           ^1.48M
  188|  23.8M|        for x in 0..4 {
                               ^5.95M
  189|  23.8M|            let v = i32::from(pred[y * stride + x]) + residual[y * 4 + x];
  190|  23.8M|            pred[y * stride + x] = v.clamp(0, 255) as u8;
  191|  23.8M|        }
  192|       |    }
  193|  1.48M|}
  194|       |
  195|       |/// The 8x8 inverse quantisation scale matrix classes (spec 8.5.12.1,
  196|       |/// `v8x8` rows of Table 7-4 for each `qP % 6`). The class index per
  197|       |/// 8x8 raster position comes from [`scale_class8`].
  198|       |const LEVEL_SCALE_8X8: [[u16; 6]; 6] = [
  199|       |    [20, 18, 32, 19, 25, 24],
  200|       |    [22, 19, 35, 21, 28, 26],
  201|       |    [26, 23, 42, 24, 33, 31],
  202|       |    [28, 25, 45, 26, 35, 33],
  203|       |    [32, 28, 51, 30, 40, 38],
  204|       |    [36, 32, 58, 34, 46, 43],
  205|       |];
  206|       |
  207|       |/// Raster position -> 8x8 `LevelScale8x8` class (0..5), the pattern
  208|       |/// derived from spec 8.5.12.1's scale-position mapping (matches the
  209|       |/// reference decoder's `dequant8_coeff_init_scan`).
  210|       |const SCALE8_CLASS: [u8; 64] = [
  211|       |    0, 3, 4, 3, 0, 3, 4, 3, 3, 1, 5, 1, 3, 1, 5, 1, 4, 5, 2, 5, 4, 5, 2, 5, 3, 1, 5, 1, 3, 1, 5, 1,
  212|       |    0, 3, 4, 3, 0, 3, 4, 3, 3, 1, 5, 1, 3, 1, 5, 1, 4, 5, 2, 5, 4, 5, 2, 5, 3, 1, 5, 1, 3, 1, 5, 1,
  213|       |];
  214|       |
  215|       |/// Dequantises one 8x8 coefficient block in place (spec 8.5.12.1 with
  216|       |/// the flat-16 scaling list). The multiplicand is
  217|       |/// `LevelScale8x8(qP%6, i, j) * 16 << (qP/6)` divided by 64 — the same
  218|       |/// folded form the reference decoder applies at coefficient-decode
  219|       |/// time (`(c * qmul + 32) >> 6`). Products go through i64.
  220|  73.7k|pub(crate) fn dequant_8x8(coeffs: &mut [i32; 64], qp: u8) {
  221|  73.7k|    debug_assert!(qp < 52);
  222|  73.7k|    let m = LEVEL_SCALE_8X8[QP_MOD6[qp as usize] as usize];
  223|  73.7k|    let q6 = qp as i64 / 6;
  224|  4.72M|    for i in 0..64 {
                           ^73.7k
  225|  4.72M|        let qmul = (i64::from(m[SCALE8_CLASS[i] as usize]) * 16) << q6;
  226|  4.72M|        let c = i64::from(coeffs[i]);
  227|  4.72M|        // (c * qmul + 32) >> 6 with sign-correct rounding toward -inf,
  228|  4.72M|        // matching the reference decoder's arithmetic exactly.
  229|  4.72M|        let v = (c * qmul + 32) >> 6;
  230|  4.72M|        coeffs[i] = v.clamp(i32::MIN as i64, i32::MAX as i64) as i32;
  231|  4.72M|    }
  232|  73.7k|}
  233|       |
  234|       |/// 8x4 block of i32 (raster order).
  235|       |pub(crate) type Block8 = [i32; 64];
  236|       |
  237|       |/// One 8x8 inverse-transform butterfly pass on the row or column
  238|       |/// picked by `idx` (spec 8.5.12.3, the reference decoder's exact
  239|       |/// shift-sharing implementation — the `>>1`/`>>2` sub-terms are
  240|       |/// mandatory, they are not factorisations).
  241|  1.18M|fn idct8_pass(b: &mut [i32], idx: [usize; 8]) {
  242|  1.18M|    let c = [
  243|  1.18M|        b[idx[0]], b[idx[1]], b[idx[2]], b[idx[3]], b[idx[4]], b[idx[5]], b[idx[6]], b[idx[7]],
  244|  1.18M|    ];
  245|  1.18M|    let a0 = c[0].wrapping_add(c[4]);
  246|  1.18M|    let a2 = c[0].wrapping_sub(c[4]);
  247|  1.18M|    let a4 = (c[2] >> 1).wrapping_sub(c[6]);
  248|  1.18M|    let a6 = (c[6] >> 1).wrapping_add(c[2]);
  249|  1.18M|    let b0 = a0.wrapping_add(a6);
  250|  1.18M|    let b2 = a2.wrapping_add(a4);
  251|  1.18M|    let b4 = a2.wrapping_sub(a4);
  252|  1.18M|    let b6 = a0.wrapping_sub(a6);
  253|  1.18M|    let a1 = c[3]
  254|  1.18M|        .wrapping_neg()
  255|  1.18M|        .wrapping_add(c[5])
  256|  1.18M|        .wrapping_sub(c[7])
  257|  1.18M|        .wrapping_sub(c[7] >> 1);
  258|  1.18M|    let a3 = c[1]
  259|  1.18M|        .wrapping_add(c[7])
  260|  1.18M|        .wrapping_sub(c[3])
  261|  1.18M|        .wrapping_sub(c[3] >> 1);
  262|  1.18M|    let a5 = c[1]
  263|  1.18M|        .wrapping_neg()
  264|  1.18M|        .wrapping_add(c[7])
  265|  1.18M|        .wrapping_add(c[5])
  266|  1.18M|        .wrapping_add(c[5] >> 1);
  267|  1.18M|    let a7 = c[3]
  268|  1.18M|        .wrapping_add(c[5])
  269|  1.18M|        .wrapping_add(c[1])
  270|  1.18M|        .wrapping_add(c[1] >> 1);
  271|  1.18M|    let b1 = (a7 >> 2).wrapping_add(a1);
  272|  1.18M|    let b3 = a3.wrapping_add(a5 >> 2);
  273|  1.18M|    let b5 = (a3 >> 2).wrapping_sub(a5);
  274|  1.18M|    let b7 = a7.wrapping_sub(a1 >> 2);
  275|  1.18M|    b[idx[0]] = b0.wrapping_add(b7);
  276|  1.18M|    b[idx[7]] = b0.wrapping_sub(b7);
  277|  1.18M|    b[idx[1]] = b2.wrapping_add(b5);
  278|  1.18M|    b[idx[6]] = b2.wrapping_sub(b5);
  279|  1.18M|    b[idx[2]] = b4.wrapping_add(b3);
  280|  1.18M|    b[idx[5]] = b4.wrapping_sub(b3);
  281|  1.18M|    b[idx[3]] = b6.wrapping_add(b1);
  282|  1.18M|    b[idx[4]] = b6.wrapping_sub(b1);
  283|  1.18M|}
  284|       |
  285|       |/// The H.264 8x8 integer inverse transform (spec 8.5.12.3): row pass,
  286|       |/// then column pass, matching the reference decoder's `idct8_add`
  287|       |/// exactly — the passes are NOT commutative because each 1-D pass
  288|       |/// contains rounding shifts (`>>1`/`>>2`), and the reference folds the
  289|       |/// rounding constant into the DC before the passes.
  290|  73.7k|pub(crate) fn inverse_8x8(b: &mut Block8) {
  291|       |    // The reference decoder adds the rounding constant into the DC
  292|       |    // coefficient BEFORE the passes (h264idct_template.c idct8_add:
  293|       |    // `block[0] += 32;`) and stores with a plain arithmetic `>> 6`.
  294|       |    // These are NOT interchangeable with a store-time `+32` because the
  295|       |    // `>>1`/`>>2` shifts inside the butterflies round differently.
  296|  73.7k|    b[0] = b[0].wrapping_add(32);
  297|       |    // Rows first, columns second: combined with the ZIGZAG_8X8 storage
  298|       |    // (the transpose of the reference decoder's block layout) this
  299|       |    // reproduces `idct8_add` bit-exactly (see tables.rs note).
  300|   590k|    for i in 0..8 {
                           ^73.7k
  301|   590k|        let idx = [
  302|   590k|            i * 8,
  303|   590k|            i * 8 + 1,
  304|   590k|            i * 8 + 2,
  305|   590k|            i * 8 + 3,
  306|   590k|            i * 8 + 4,
  307|   590k|            i * 8 + 5,
  308|   590k|            i * 8 + 6,
  309|   590k|            i * 8 + 7,
  310|   590k|        ];
  311|   590k|        idct8_pass(b, idx);
  312|   590k|    }
  313|   590k|    for i in 0..8 {
                           ^73.7k
  314|   590k|        let idx = [i, 8 + i, 16 + i, 24 + i, 32 + i, 40 + i, 48 + i, 56 + i];
  315|   590k|        idct8_pass(b, idx);
  316|   590k|    }
  317|       |    // Final normalisation: plain arithmetic shift (the rounding
  318|       |    // constant was already folded into the DC above).
  319|  4.72M|    for v in b.iter_mut() {
                           ^73.7k^73.7k
  320|  4.72M|        *v >>= 6;
  321|  4.72M|    }
  322|  73.7k|}
  323|       |
  324|       |/// Adds an 8x8 residual block to a prediction region (`stride`

    1|       |//! Regenerates and verifies `reference.json`: the hex-exact decoded-plane
    2|       |//! digests of the pith suite's H.264 lane. One vector per conformance
    3|       |//! fixture under `tests/fixtures/`, in deterministic (sorted) corpus
    4|       |//! order, pinning the aggregate SHA-256 over every decoded frame's
    5|       |//! `y || cb || cr` planes in presentation order.
    6|       |//!
    7|       |//! `gen-reference gen` rewrites the file; `gen-reference verify`
    8|       |//! recomputes it and fails on any difference. CI runs `verify` so a
    9|       |//! decoder change that shifts output bytes cannot land silently, and CD
   10|       |//! ships the file with the SDK artifacts as the cross-language oracle.
   11|       |//!
   12|       |//! The corpus is byte-stable across platforms by construction: the
   13|       |//! decoder is integer-only (`#![no_std]`, no floats in the output path)
   14|       |//! and the digests are computed from decoded planes only — never from
   15|       |//! encoder state.
   16|       |
